Did You Know You Have a Birth Month Winter Bird? Here's All 12 and Their Special Meanings

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Beautiful plants and ideas to brighten your garden.

Expert tips for growing fruit, vegetables and herbs at home.

Sign up to get fresh gardening ideas, inspiration and expert advice delivered straight to your inbox.

The 2-Minute Lard Trick That Helps Smaller Songbirds Get Their Fair Share of Food in Fall and Winter

Why Birds Peck Windows in Fall – the Real Reasons Revealed by Experts

These 8 Evergreen Plants Make Your Porch Look Magical at Christmas – And They Stay Gorgeous All Winter

Sign up for the Gardening Know How newsletter today and receive a free copy of our e-book "How to Grow Delicious Tomatoes".

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

As temperatures dip and the nights draw in, why not look to your birth month winter bird to learn more about your personality?

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The seasons are shifting, which means our feathered friends are about to embark upon a changing of the guard... so which is your birth month winter bird, you ask?

It's not a silly question, by any means. After all, some birds migrate south for winter, some arrive from further north, and year-round residents shift their habits. And, from the brilliant red cardinal perched against a snowy backdrop to the tiny dark-eyed junco hopping beneath a bird feeder, these seasonal visitors have plenty of personality.

With that in mind, then, what if every month had its own winter bird to call its own? What might it reveal about the people born under its wings?

Now, we’re already familiar with the idea of a classic birth month bird, not to mention the birth month owl and birth month bird of prey, but what might the unique traits that make each winter bird so wonderful say about our own personalities?

Some are bold and charismatic, others are quiet and contemplative, and a few are wonderfully independent. All, though, are beautiful in their own special way, and so much fun to watch as they go about their business over the colder months.

With that in mind, then, I've paired 12 fascinating winter birds with the personality traits commonly associated with each birth month, along with a few easy ways you can make your garden a more welcoming place for them. Time to grab yourself a motion-activated wildlife camera with night vision, like the GardePro on Amazon, then, and start reading...

If you're born in January, you're likely to be headstrong, disciplined and hardworking (all of which are, natural leadership qualities, just in case you fancied informing your boss). Naturally, then, your birth month winter bird is a wonderful reflection of that determination: the snow bunting.

Sign up for the Gardening Know How newsletter today and receive a free copy of our e-book "How to Grow Delicious Tomatoes".

These beautiful little birds are highly social during winter, often gathering in flocks to forage for seeds in open fields and along shorelines, while their ability to thrive in bitterly cold conditions is rather impressive for such a small bird.

Like January babies, basically, they don't shy away from a challenge; they meet it head-on! To help them do this, be sure to leave native grasses and seed-producing plants standing where possible, rather than tidying every corner of your garden.

One of the most iconic birds in the world, the striking snowy owl has brilliant white plumage, piercing yellow eyes and an unmistakable presence. They spend much of the year in the Arctic, but some travel south during winter, occasionally appearing in northern parts of the United States (although they tend to favour wide-open landscapes, including coastal dunes, fields and other exposed areas where they can hunt).

Often solitary hunters, February's birth month winter bird gets by on its own instincts and remarkable ability to spot prey from a distance. It's also wonderfully adapted to its harsh environment, making it a fitting symbol of independence, deep-thinking, and inner strength.

To help snowy owls out over the colder months, avoid using rodenticides that can poison birds of prey, turn your garden into an owl hangout (it only takes five plants), and always give any owl you encounter plenty of space.

March babies are often associated with introspection, creativity, compassion and a thoughtful nature. It makes a whole lot of sense, then, that their birth month winter bird is the dark-eyed junco, a small but charming visitor that brings plenty of life to colder gardens.

Sometimes nicknamed the snowbird, dark-eyed juncos tend to spend much of their time quietly hopping around on the ground, searching for fallen seeds beneath trees and feeders. As such, they're a lovely match for March personalities: observant, gentle and happy to enjoy the quieter things in life.

If you fancy seeing more in your garden, remember that juncos are particularly fond of feeding on the ground, so provide a sheltered feeding area with small seeds. Leaving native grasses and seed heads standing also gives them more natural foraging opportunities.

If you're an April baby, you're thought to be charismatic, energetic, straightforward and naturally confident. As such, there's perhaps no better bird to represent you than the unmistakable blue jay.

With its brilliant blue plumage, distinctive crest and loud calls, the blue jay certainly knows how to make itself noticed. These intelligent members of the crow family have bold personalities that often make them one of the most entertaining birds to watch, in fact.

Blue jays are also clever problem-solvers, not to mention never afraid to make their presence known. Plant native oak trees and other nut-producing species to provide natural food and shelter to help them out, and remember that they also enjoy unsalted peanuts , so you can occasionally offer some in a suitable feeder.

I know, I know; a wild turkey might not feel like the most glamorous birth month winter bird, but hear me out! May personalities are often described as sociable, talented, loyal and strong-willed. And wild turkeys? Why, they're highly social, often gathering in flocks during winter as they search for food, not to mention putting on some impressive courtship displays.

They're also resourceful foragers, searching through woodland floors for seeds, nuts, berries and insects. During colder months, they can be spotted scratching through leaf litter and searching beneath trees for whatever food they can find. To help yours out, then, take care to leave natural cover in place where possible, and avoid using pesticides that reduce the insects and other natural food sources they rely on.

Known for its melancholy-sounding coo, the gentle mourning dove is a familiar sight across much of the United States, often seen perched on telephone wires, fences and tree branches, or quietly searching the ground for seeds.

They're also known for their strong pair bonds, with partners often working together to raise their young, so it's easy to see why this bird suits June babies (especially as they tend to be such romantic dreamers). It's a birth month winter bird that perfectly aligns with all of that sensitivity and imagination.

Remember, mourning doves enjoy feeding on the ground, so provide a clean feeding area with something like sunflower hearts if you want to see them in your garden. Plant some native seed-producing plants and shrubs, too, to help provide natural food and shelter.

July babies are adventurous free spirits known for their cheerful and (ahem) slightly slightly eccentric personalities, not to mention their strong protective streaks when it comes to family. It was inevitable, then, that the snow goose would prove their birth month winter bird.

Known for their impressive journeys between breeding and wintering grounds, snow geese are often spotted travelling in enormous flocks that fill the sky with their distinctive calls. They are also strongly connected to family life; young birds travel with their parents, and family groups can remain together during migration and winter.

If you want to help your feathery soulmate out, remember that snow geese depend on healthy wetlands, grasslands and agricultural landscapes during migration and winter. Supporting wetland conservation and protecting natural resting and feeding areas, then, is one of the best ways to keeo them thriving.

It makes so much sense that August babies get the delightful red-breasted nuthatch as their birth month winter bird, as it sums up all their best qualities perfectly. Yes, this little bird might be small, but it certainly doesn't lack confidence. One of its most entertaining habits is climbing headfirst down tree trunks, something it does with remarkable ease as it searches for insects and seeds hidden in the bark.

During autumn and winter, red-breasted nuthatches also collect and store conifer seeds, helping ensure they have food available when conditions become more challenging. They're hardworking, resourceful and rather determined, making them a lovely match for August birthdays.

To help them out, try to plant native trees such as pine, spruce and fir where appropriate for your region. You can also offer sunflower seeds, peanuts or suet in a clean feeder, particularly during colder weather.

Intelligent, spiritual, organized and analytical, September babies tend to be more successful in life (genuinely; look it up!) and thus are represented by the pine siskin.

These small finches might be easy to overlook at first, but they're fascinating little birds. They are adaptable, clever, and brimming with plenty of resourcefulness, though, which is why you often seen clinging to the tips of pine branches and even hanging upside down as they extract seeds from cones.

Sure, they're rather unpredictable visitors, as their movements depend heavily on food availability; this means that some gardens might welcome large flocks one winter and see very few the next. To keep them flocking, grow native conifers and other seed-producing plants to provide natural food, as well as nyjer (thistle) seed and sunflower hearts in finch feeders.

As an October baby myself, I'm more than happy to see this charming wanderer as my birth month winter bird! Especially as, with its striking yellow, black and white plumage and enormous pale bill, this chunky finch is one of North America's most distinctive birds.

Evening grosbeaks are adventurous travellers, sometimes moving considerable distances in search of food during autumn and winter. They're also wonderfully sociable, often gathering in groups around food sources and making quite an impression when they arrive at a backyard feeder.

For October birthdays, they're a lovely symbol of persistence, confidence and a little good fortune. After all, spotting one can feel like winning a small birdwatching lottery (although you can boost your chances with a handful of black-oil sunflower seeds, which can be offered in a sturdy platform feeder).

Empathetic, generous, brave and deeply determined, November-born souls are represented by one of North America's most magnificent waterbirds: the trumpeter swan.

These enormous white swans are impressive in every sense, with long necks, powerful wings and a distinctive black bill. They are known for their strong pair bonds and devoted parenting, and their family groups can be particularly striking to see gliding across a winter lake. In fact, it's their loyalty, protective instincts and graceful strength that make them a beautiful match for November birthdays.

These aren't traditional garden birds, so you can help them by protecting local wetlands, ponds and freshwater habitats, and always taking care to avoid disturbing swans when they're resting or feeding.

Generous, patriotic, optimistic and willing to take a few risks? Yeah, obviously the birth month winter bird for December is the cardinal!

Known for their strong pair bonds and the way they communicate through a variety of musical calls, their cheerful appearance against bare branches and snowy gardens has led to them becoming something not unlike a symbol of hope during the festive season. In fact, many believe that if they're the first bird you spot on January 1st, you're owed 12 months of love, optimism, and luck for the year ahead.

If you want to help yours out, plant native berry-producing shrubs such as winterberry, serviceberry and dogwood to make your garden a one-stop destination for food, shelter, and nesting opportunities.

Whether you believe in the concept of a birth month winter bird or not, there's no denying that they serve as a lovely reminder of just how much wildlife is around us, even during the coldest months of the year.

And perhaps the best part is that you don't have to wait for your own birth month to enjoy these feathered visitors. By planting native trees and shrubs, leaving seed heads standing, providing clean water and maintaining safe feeding areas, you can help make your garden a welcoming winter refuge for your feathered friends throughout the year.

Give it a go, I say! You'll do your bit for local wildlife and you might even discover a new favorite feathered friend along the way...

Kayleigh is an enthusiastic (sometimes too enthusiastic!) gardener and has worked in media for over a decade. She previously served as digital editor at Stylist magazine, and has written extensively for Ideal Home, Woman & Home, Homes & Gardens, and a handful of other titles. Kayleigh is passionate about wildlife-friendly gardening, and recently cancelled her weekend plans to build a mini pond when her toddler found a frog living in their water barrel. As such, her garden – designed around the stunning magnolia tree at its centre – is filled to the brim with pollinator-friendly blooms, homemade bird feeders, and old logs for insects to nest in.

One gigabyte of Wikipedia losslessly compressed to 105.39MB with self-learning AI transformer model

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

A dozen hybrid compressors can beat its score, but none relies on a neural network alone.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Tufa Labs’ tufazip has compressed 1GB of information from Wikipedia into just 105MB of space, losslessly, without using any hand-built models, according to a research post. The method uses a 163-million-parameter Transformer model as a predictor instead, and similar compression or decompression would take two Nvidia B200 GPUs about a week to manage. It’s the smallest result on the Large Text Compression Benchmark from any entry that uses a neural network as its only predictor.

We shrank 1 GB of Wikipedia to 105.39 MB, losslessly. Introducing tufazip: the best entry on the Large Text Compression Benchmark using a neural network as its sole predictor. Everything above it mixes a transformer with hand-built models.November 19, 2026

Researcher Tommy He built tufazip, and the full package, at 105.39MB after optimization, includes a small, 4.33MB pre-trained starter model — stored at low precision, or quantized, to save space — that’s needed to unpack the file. The full model is a 12-layer Transformer, which means there are 12 stages for each guess to go through. The starter stores just two already-trained stages, copying them back and forth to fill all 12 once the full model spins up in memory; each copy keeps learning on its own.

This doesn’t work like a traditional chatbot or cloud AI but is, rather, a self-contained language model that learns on the fly. Notable here is that this compression is lossless, which means there’s no risk of any data being lost in the process. This means that predictor updates must be “fully deterministic.”

Training happens in two stages. The starter, a 34-million-parameter model, is trained offline, before any compression begins. Then, the full model begins to learn online, by predicting each token and training on it, with the unpacking side repeating the same steps, with the model itself being rebuilt alongside the text. For this reason, the total size must include, besides the compressed text, only the unpacking program and the files it needs, including the starter, but not the full trained model.

There is no traditional, hand-built compression model that assists, setting it apart, Tufa Labs says, from all 12 entries that now rank above it on the benchmark, which mix a Transformer with hand-built models. Its first, online-only version ranked second when submitted in early September; the 105.39MB version was 13th as of the benchmark’s Nov. 19 update. Emphasis should be placed on this being a text-only benchmark, for those imagining media files.

While this sounds more exciting than, say, WinRAR, it’s for research purposes and not a product, and that’s underlined by what you’d need to actually run it. Currently, two Nvidia B200 GPUs — plus two Intel Xeon Platinum 8570 CPUs in the test machine — would need 631,548 seconds to compress and 645,748 seconds to decompress the 105.39MB version, or about 7.3 and 7.5 days, respectively, not counting the starter’s offline training.

The first version, which learned entirely online and came in at 105.98MB, took 647,595 and 630,136 seconds. Its GPUs peaked at 43.37 GiB of VRAM between them, and total memory use, GPUs included, peaked at roughly 68,525 MiB during compression and 73,748 MiB during decompression.

Get Tom's Hardware's best news and in-depth reviews, straight to your inbox.

The experiment is fundamentally interesting because it shows, for example, that a smaller vocabulary can actually help: a 4K-word tokenizer beats both 8K and 16K. Giving Wikipedia’s XML markup its own dedicated tokens made the compression worse. Bigger isn’t always better, and that goes for model size, too, as a 267-million-parameter model did slightly worse than a 188-million-parameter model on this data set. With a smaller, 100MB version of the file, gains leveled off at about 77 million parameters. A run started from a bigger, six-layer offline model predicted better, with just a 99.17MB payload, but its chunkier weights made the total file larger than the online-only run.

Improving compression can be costly, as widening the window of already-processed text used for extra training, from 15 to 35 million tokens, only cut the payload 1.21% at the cost of 40% more compute. The key takeaways are that optimization isn’t always as simple as throwing more at the problem, and that trade-offs can be more expensive than expected. He frames that six-layer trade-off as the next research question: how much predictive quality a smaller model can keep, which makes the vanishing point a balance between predictive quality and model size for this benchmark.

Harry Kane is targeting the "business end of the season" after undergoing surgery on a torn ligament in his left ankle.

Kane wrote on social media: "The highs and lows of life! Obviously very disappointed to get this injury but as always it's an opportunity to show resilience and improvement going into 2027.

If AI Chips Become Commoditized, Nvidia Has the Most to Lose, While These 3 Stocks Are Set to Gain

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Nvidia has a lot to lose as AI chips become fungible, while these stocks could gain.

By Geoffrey Seiler – Nov 21, 2026 at 6:15AM EST

The AI chip market is starting to shift, and with it, Nvidia's (NVDA -14.01%) grip. Anthropic recently revealed that its Claude Mythos model could start running part of its AI models on alternative AI chips to Nvidia's graphics processing units (GPUs) in just a few days with basically no human intervention, a process that used to take six to 12 months and dozens of engineers. This greatly reduces Nvidia's CUDA moat at a time when competitors are starting to catch up.

Alphabet's (GOOGL -2.70%) (GOOG -2.59%) Tensor Processing Units (TPUs) have started to outperform Nvidia's GPUs on a performance-per-dollar basis, according to Semianalysis (though some dispute the results). Meanwhile, Advanced Micro Devices' (AMD -13.25%) GPUs continue to gain traction. The company has improved its software stack, and its chips' larger memory capacity makes them ideal for inference.

If the gap between AI chips continues to shrink and AI models can be quickly shifted to run on any AI accelerator, this is very bad news for Nvidia. Not only will the company continue to lose market share, but, more importantly, its pricing power will erode, and its margins will shrink. The Nvidia premium can be seen in its gross margins, which are in the mid-70% range, versus AMD’s in the mid-50% range. Meanwhile, all of Nvidia's largest customers, outside of SpaceX (SPCX -7.69%), have been developing their own custom AI chips to try to break free of Nvidia's high prices.

All is not lost for Nvidia, as the company still holds some advantages. It has created a cohesive hardware, software, and networking ecosystem that should still be able to command a premium. It also has major current supply chain advantages, having locked up more access to constrained high bandwidth memory (HBM) supply and having more manufacturing and packaging capacity with Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing (TSM -9.48%). That will help it maintain its leadership position, even as it will likely face continued market-share and pricing pressure.

While Nvidia may struggle, these three AI stocks look set to benefit.

Lower AI accelerator prices and more Nvidia GPU alternatives could boost overall AI chip demand, which would benefit Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing. It would also lower TSMC's customer concentration and could actually help with pricing power and gross margins.

Whether a hyperscaler, neocloud, enterprise, or government buys Nvidia GPUs, AMD accelerators, or ASICs through a company like Broadcom (AVGO -12.19%) or Marvell Technology (MRVL -11.69%), TSMC still wins at the end of the day. That makes the stock a buy.

As both a cloud computing giant and a developer of TPUs, Alphabet is one of the best-positioned companies to benefit from the commoditization of AI chips. Alphabet has more than a decade's head start with custom chips and is creating an ecosystem to rival chipmakers like Nvidia and AMD. This lets it get better utilization out of its data centers and gives it an advantage over other companies using their own custom AI chips.

Meanwhile, because its offerings cost much less than Nvidia's, it should be able to gain share with external customers without sacrificing margins. This ultimately could make Alphabet one of the biggest winners in the AI space during this transition.

Starting from a much smaller market-share base with an operating model already built around slimmer gross margins, AMD should benefit as AI chips become more commoditized. Between its lower prices, improving cost performance, and the recent introduction of a complete AI rack system (Helios), AI chip pricing normalization and chip fungibility actually become a tailwind rather than a headwind for a smaller Nvidia competitor like AMD.

Meanwhile, the market is heading in AMD's direction with inference and agentic AI, with customers caring far more about memory capacity, central processing unit (CPU) integrations, and cost-per-action, which is where its chips shine. With its new Helios system, which combines its GPUs and CPUs, AMD has a big opportunity to establish itself as a meaningful alternative to Nvidia in the coming years. This makes the stock a buy.

Marion Cotillard Says ‘I’m Not Here to Judge Movies’ as Marrakech Jury President and ‘Doesn’t Want Any Information’ Before Watching: ‘Like a Virgin’

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Marion Cotillard, who serves as the jury president at this year’s Marrakech Film Festival, says she prefers watching films with “as little information as possible.”

“I don’t know anything about the movies we’re gonna see, I haven’t seen a single image,” the Oscar-winning French actress said at the festival’s jury press conference on Saturday morning. “That’s how I love to see movies, I don’t want to have any information.” Related Stories Peter Alsted’s Animal Conservation Doc ‘Dr. Hedgehog’ Boarded by Autlook Filmsales Ahead of IDFA Forum (EXCLUSIVE) What Happens When Documentary Makers Sell Their Footage to AI Aggregators?

“I want to be like a virgin,” she quipped.

Ironically, she said she doesn’t think her role on the jury is to “judge movies.” “I consider myself as part of an audience: I’m here to be touched, to be taken by stories.”

Cotillard was also asked about “Inception,” in which she starred alongside Leonardo DiCaprio. Does she understand Christopher Nolan’s famously complex film? “I don’t know…I thought I had,” she said to laughter. “Maybe I have. And maybe I haven’t.”

The nine-member jury includes “Sex Education” and “Barbie” star Emma Mackey; “Phantom Thread” standout Vicky Krieps; Danish actor Claes Bang, who featured in Ruben Östlund’s Palme d’Or winner “The Square”; French actor-turned-director director Gilles Lellouche, known for “Beating Hearts”; “The Voice of Hind Rajab” helmer Kaouther Ben Hania; “All of Us Strangers” filmmaker Andrew Haigh; Franco-Senegalese helmer Ramata-Toulaye Sy; and Moroccan director-producer Saïd Hamich Benlarbi.

The jury will award the festival’s Étoile d’Or to one of the 14 films in competition, which consists of first and second features.

Cotillard said her only expectation she has in helping to select this year’s competition winner is “to be shaken, to be moved.”

“When I can watch a movie and I can laugh and smile and be questioned at the same time,” she continued. “When there’s a story that on the paper I wouldn’t be very interested [in] and the filmmaker who tells the story will open a new vision for me.”

She summed it up simply: “How do you judge art anyway?”

Mackey agrees with Cotillard that her role on the jury is to “receive the films as a spectator. “We’re here to learn, its very pure somehow,” the “Sex Education” star said. “I don’t pretend that I know everything and I’m here to learn and to absorb these films as as a cinema lover.”

“It’s an honor and I’m happy to spend a week watching two films a day, that’s amazing,” she added.

Jury members were also asked about the relatively strong female representation at Marrakech and its broader implications for gender equality in the industry. This year, women hold a majority of seats on the nine-member jury while six of the 14 films in competition were directed by women.

Krieps drew on her role as an Irish maid in Ryan Murphy’s “Monster: The Lizzie Borden Story” to reflect on the implications of this type of female representation on-screen. “I think women have had the role of oppressed and silent,” she said. “Often we had to hide, we had to disappear because otherwise we weren’t safe.”

“I think the role I play in ‘Monster’ is someone who becomes very visible in a time when people were supposed to be invisible,” she continued, adding that her character’s wild red hair and contemporary costuming could “help people see differently in the future.”

Sy, meanwhile, offered a more succinct response, suggesting that true progress will come when female representation is no longer a point of discussion.

“The day when we stop counting how many women there are in juries, in jobs, as directors, then it will be normal,” the Sengalese filmmaker said to enthusiastic applause. “We have to stop asking the question and counting how many there are.”

On Friday night, the festival opened with Jesse Eisenberg’s “The Debut,” a musical comedy starring Julianne Moore as a shy New Jersey housewife who auditions for a regional production. Though Eisenberg and Moore weren’t in attendance, the opening ceremony was still a starry affair with guests including Tilda Swinton and “Knives Out” director Rian Johnson, who is giving a talk at the festival on Sunday.

Mackey is next playing the White Witch in Greta Gerwig’s “Narnia: The Magician’s Nephew,” and told Variety on the red carpet that she was excited to meet Swinton — who played the role in the original film trilogy — for the first time at Marrakech.

“It’s thrilling, my absolute dream person,” she said, adding: “She’s the queen. I wouldn’t even know how to breathe around her, I don’t think.”

The Marrakech Film Festival runs through Nov. 28.

L'ex Jungdal: “Allenarsi con Maignan e Donnarumma è stata la miglior scuola possibile. Tresoldi? Un grande. Prendetelo!”

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Quanti possono dire di essersi allenati al fianco di Gianluigi Donnarumma e Mike Maignan quando erano ancora giovanissimi? Andreas Jungdal è uno di questi. Arrivato al Milan nel 2019 quando aveva appena 17 anni, il portiere danese ha avuto il privilegio di condividere il campo con due dei migliori estremi difensori della sua generazione, imparando anche da una leggenda rossonera come Nelson Dida. Oggi, dopo aver intrapreso il proprio percorso lontano da Milano, continua a seguire con affetto il club che tifava da bambino. In esclusiva ai microfoni di MilanNews, nell’intervista realizzata dal giornalista Alessandro Schiavone, l'attuale portiere titolare del K.V.C Westerlo in Belgio e riserva della nazionale danese ripercorre gli anni in rossonero, mette a confronto Donnarumma e Maignan, racconta il rapporto con Dida e si sofferma anche su Nicolò Tresoldi, l’attaccante italo-tedesco che sta brillando in Belgio e che consiglierebbe senza esitazioni al Milan. Protagonista di una grande serata, con un rigore parato. Hai imparato da Gigio come si parano i rigori? “Sì, si può dire così (ride, ndr). Vabbè, ho imparato tanto da Gigio e pure da Mike. Insomma, da tutti i portieri del Milan! Allenarmi con loro due è stata una vera scuola per portieri.”   Hai imparato di più da Maignan o da Donnarumma?  “Vabbè, sono due portieri molto diversi. Secondo me Gigio sulla linea è un portiere incredibile, mentre Mike magari con la palla è più forte. Hanno qualità diverse e sono stato fortunato a imparare da tutti e due.”   Ti sei allenato due anni con Donnarumma, dal 2019 al 2021, e 18 mesi con Mike Maignan, dall'estate 2021 al gennaio 2023. Puoi dirci chi è più forte?  “Mah, io non posso dire chi è più forte (ride, ndr).  Ma secondo me sulla linea Gigio è il numero uno al mondo! Ha un fisico enorme e un’esplosività impressionante. Però Mike è al Milan da oltre cinque anni ormai e ha vinto pure il campionato! Anche qui parliamo di un campione e lo ha dimostrato anche con la nazionale francese, facendo molto, molto bene…”   In cuor tuo, quando Donnarumma decise di non rinnovare il contratto e di andare al PSG a parametro zero, non speravi di diventare il portiere titolare del Milan?  “Sì, certo che ci speravo, ma ero molto, molto giovane. Poi ero il terzo o quarto portiere. Se vuoi diventare il titolare del Milan devi essere un campione! Hanno preso Mike, ma io sono felice dei miei anni in rossonero. Va bene così.”   La scuola italiana dei portieri è così forte come si dice?  “L’Italia ha fatto la storia sfornando portieri di grande livello, soprattutto tecnicamente. Lì sono fissati con la tecnica… come pari la palla e come muovi i piedi. E tutte quelle cose lì… Credo che per un portiere giovane quella italiana sia una scuola fantastica."   Ma come ti ha scoperto il Milan?  "Penso tramite lo scouting… Io avevo mosso i primi passi al Vejle. Ho fatto un anno in nazionale nell’Under 17 e penso che mi abbiano scoperto lì. Ma sinceramente non sono sicurissimo. Sono stato fortunato ad andare lì e seguo tuttora il Milan. È il club per il quale tifavo da piccolo. È un grande club a livello mondiale che tifo ancora. Auguro tutto il meglio al Milan.”   Il tuo preparatore dei portieri è stato niente meno che mister Nelson Dida, vincitore di due Champions League e portiere del Grande Milan.  “Innanzitutto è una persona straordinaria! Se non sbaglio è passato dalla Primavera alla prima squadra. Anche da lui ho imparato tantissimo, così come da Gigi Ragno ed Emiliano Betti, che erano anche loro preparatori dei portieri. Però che feeling con Dida, che era una vera e propria leggenda. Sono stati bei tempi…”   Tresoldi, tra andata e ritorno fra Bruges e Westerlo nella passata stagione, ti ha segnato tre gol. Il Milan starebbe pensando di prenderlo a gennaio: farebbe bene a puntare sull’italo-tedesco? “Assolutamente sì! Lui qui in Belgio sta facendo davvero bene, è sempre tra i capocannonieri del campionato. È ancora molto giovane, ma sta facendo dei passi impressionanti. Il Milan farà bene a prenderlo.”   È quel tipo di attaccante che magari non vedi per 89 minuti e partecipa poco al gioco... e poi al 90’ la butta dentro. Sei d’accordo?  “Esatto… quando deve essere lì, è là… davanti alla porta. In fase di realizzazione è davvero forte! Il Bruges ha una squadra forte che lo manda sempre in porta! Nicolò è davvero forte!”   Quando vedi Torriani giocare in prima squadra di tanto in tanto, non provi un po’ d’invidia? Magari con un pizzico di fortuna in più avresti potuto esserci tu al suo posto…  “Sì, ma nel calcio, così come nella vita, le cose vanno come devono andare. Lui ha avuto anche adesso delle occasioni. Sono felice per lui, anche perché abbiamo fatto un po’ lo stesso percorso nel Milan. Quando lo vedo giocare sono felice per lui.”   Sogni di tornare al Milan da protagonista un giorno? Magari come un certo Dida che dopo la papera di Leeds lasciò in prestito prima di tornare e diventare il miglior portiere al mondo dal 2002 al 2005...   “Mah, certo! È un sogno e ogni giocatore sogna di giocare per una grande squadra come il Milan. È un sogno che ho da bambino… Forza Milan!”

Chicago Mayor Brandon Johnson fights to stay on 2027 ballot amid City Hall exodus, credit warning

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Johnson, Tobin reports, could be kicked off the ballot for re-election.

Chicago Mayor Brandon Johnson (D) is fighting to keep his name on the 2027 ballot at the exact moment his administration is engulfed in a fiscal and leadership meltdown at City Hall.

Election officials are reviewing challenges to more than 25,000 of Johnson's nominating signatures ahead of the Feb. 23, 2027, mayoral election — a threshold fight unfolding just days after his third chief financial officer in 10 months abruptly resigned, Standard & Poor's placed Chicago's credit rating on negative watch and the City Council voted 45-5 to investigate three years of city financial reporting for possible misstatements.

Despite the mounting pressure, Johnson brushed off questions about whether he will survive the ballot challenge.

"Yeah, feeling just fine, I'm going to be on the ballot," Johnson told ABC 7 Chicago about the challenge. "Looking forward to building the safest, most affordable big city in America, and we're going to continue to do that work."

CHICAGO'S 12-LANGUAGE BALLOTS MAKE ELECTIONS 'HARDER TO MONITOR,' ILLINOIS GOP CHAIR WARNS

Chicago Mayor Brandon Johnson speaks to demonstrators in Grant Park who were protesting the policies of President Donald Trump and showing their support for union labor on May 01, 2025 in Chicago, Illinois. The demonstration was one of more than a thousand scheduled to take place around the country today. (Scott Olson/Getty Images)

Johnson needs 12,500 valid signatures to qualify for Chicago's Feb. 23, 2027, mayoral ballot. Hearing officers at the Chicago Board of Elections are reviewing more than 25,000 signatures challenged by supporters of perennial mayoral candidate Willie Wilson. Johnson's campaign says 6,718 signatures were not challenged and projects that he would finish with 14,788 valid signatures if the first day's 32% validation rate holds. That would put him 2,288 signatures above the requirement, but the figure is the campaign's estimate, not a final board tally. The objectors say early rulings are favoring their challenge, and the review remains unresolved.

The turmoil inside City Hall accelerated following the sudden departure of chief financial officer Ashlee Gabrysch, who said continuing in the role would "compromise my integrity and professional obligations." Johnson later said she "wasn't the right fit."

"It’s yet another high-level change in the mayor’s administration, and is a sign of continuing turmoil and a lack of direction at a very key time for the city, especially for him heading into a mayoral election," David Greising, CEO of the Better Government Association, told CBS Chicago following Gabrysch's abrupt exit.

A survey of 1,108 adults by NORC at the University of Chicago, conducted Sept. 13-23, found that 22.2% approved of Johnson's job performance, while 52.9% disapproved and 24.8% had no opinion. The survey predates both the current petition review and Gabrysch's departure.

Chicago residents are calling for more police as crime continues to rise in the Windy City. (Fox News Digital)

Meanwhile, Johnson's proposed $17.6 billion 2027 budget is intended to close an $882.4 million gap, but a 28-member City Council bloc told him it did not see the votes needed to pass his tax proposals.

Standard & Poor's ultimately placed Chicago's BBB general-obligation rating on negative CreditWatch, citing the budget stalemate and management instability. The agency said there was at least a 50% chance of a downgrade within 90 days, which could raise the city's borrowing costs.

The City Council eventually voted 45-5 on Wednesday to authorize an independent review of three years of financial reporting for possible material or intentional misstatements.

Chicago Mayor Brandon Johnson speaks during a press conference announcing the top-voted name in the city's 2026 "You Name a Snowplow" contest, "Abolish ICE," Wednesday, March 25, 2026, in Chicago (AP Photo/Erin Hooley)

They did not respond by publication.

Mamdani’s plan to dismantle NYPD’s riot unit ripped as ‘smoke and mirrors’ by former force leaders

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Former NYPD Chief John Chell and former NYPD inspector Paul Mauro are accusing New York City Mayor Zohran Mamdani's new disbanding of the Strategic Response Group as a move to please his base, with little real change to protest response being made.

New York City Mayor Zohran Mamdani's plan to gut the city’s tactical police unit is not what is seems, two former high-ranking NYPD officials tell Fox News Digital.

They argue the stunt is a political sleight of hand in which specialized officers responsible for handling violent protests will be rebranded under a different name despite the socialist mayor's promise to disband it completely.

"The problem is…day-to-day New Yorkers don't understand all this gas lighting," former NYPD Chief John Chell told Fox News Digital. "And they feel the angst of it."

"But at the end of all that, this is nothing more than smoke and mirrors," he added.

EXCLUSIVE: RETIRED POLICE SERGEANT ISSUES WARNING OVER MAMDANI'S NYPD SHAKE-UP AHEAD OF THANKSGIVING PARADE

New York City Mayor Zohran Mamdani attends a news conference at NYPD headquarters in New York City on Jan. 6, 2026. (Spencer Platt/Getty Images)

The move to dissolve the Strategic Response Group (SRG) and replace it with a "first-of-its-kind" civilian dialogue team fulfills one of Mamdani's campaign promises — noted in the first sentence of his news release on the move.

But Chell and retired NYPD inspector Paul Mauro are questioning whether it's all a facade to please the mayor's left-wing base and is a restructuring rather than a disbandment. They say the unit will likely be rebuilt under a new name to continue dealing with mass protests, major crimes and counterterrorism.

"The con here is that the SRG is simply being rebranded into an unknown name right now," Chell said.

"First of all, Mamdani did add…that there will be uniformed police standing by to take more forceful enforcement if necessary," Mauro said. "So what really changed? Well, he changed the name of that unit and he inserted this civilian unit between those uniformed cops and the protesters."

The newly proposed civilian-led Community Dialogue and De-escalation Division (CDDD) is meant to work as a buffer between protesters and the on-duty police that will remain present at large demonstrations.

"Socialism and public safety do not go hand in hand," Chell insisted.

Mamdani’s office did not respond to Fox News Digital’s request for comment on the criticism of his plan.

NY COP CALLS FOR POLICE PROTECTIONS AFTER PROTESTER HE ARRESTED SHOWED UP TO HIS HOUSE, HARASSED HIS FAMILY

NYPD officers arrest a demonstrator during an anti-ICE protest outside 26 Federal Plaza Building in New York City, New York, U.S., June 10, 2025. (Mostafa Bassim/Anadolu via Getty Images)

Both former NYPD officials warned that relying on civilians to mediate rowdy crowds could delay police intervention if a peaceful protest turns into a violent riot. This, he said, could leave officers responding to an escalating situation rather than preventing it in the first place.

"Once a cop, wherever they're assigned, becomes reactive versus proactive — public safety suffers," Chell said. "And [when cops] become reactive, they tend to hold back and that can get them hurt, and it can get civilians hurt."

"That is one of the risks inherent in this thing," Mauro added. "That you deploy the civilians, they're trying to talk…and by the time the civilians turn around and say, ‘Police, we need help,’ it may be too late."

SRG receives information from NYPD’s Intelligence and Counterterrorism Bureau to be kept aware of known or potentially dangerous demonstrations that will require specialized training to deescalate.

The SRG unit is trained to handle crowds that have turned violent, beginning with verbal de-escalation. The physical training teaches officers how to get a noncomplying individual and into police vans. A common tactic used by the SRG is bicycle crowd management where officers can line up their bikes to build a barricade in crowds, using those formations for crowd control.

"The purpose of the SRG was to make sure that you had highly trained officers who could diffuse, spin down, but then control if need be, protests that were getting out of hand," Mauro said. "That's why they were only deployed in about 6% of the instances."

MAMDANI CLASHES WITH DEMOCRATIC SOCIALISTS' PUSH TO ABOLISH POLICE AND PRISONS

In a budget hearing earlier this year it was noted the SRG did not often deal with peaceful protests, and were mostly only deployed for protests that turned violent. In 2025, NYPD reportedly facilitated 4,255 protests and the SRG was available for roughly 6% of them.

Critics of the SRG, including the ACLU's New York affiliate, say the unit is inherently violent due to its counterterrorism specialization that they claim "suppress protesters" and disregards "focus on First Amendment protections."

The training manual, reviewed by Fox News Digital, distinguishes between "peaceful/compliant" crowds and those deemed "violent/non-compliant." It also identifies three categories of protesters: "everyday citizen," "professional" and "anarchist."

The first identifier under the "everyday citizen" protester immediately acknowledges that they are honoring their First Amendment right to organize.

FLORIDA EYES POACHING NYPD OFFICERS AFTER MAMDANI DISMANTLES ELITE UNIT

The NYPD's Special Response Group's biking training manual identifies three categories of protesters: "everyday citizen," "professional" and "anarchist." (NYPD)

Despite the dismantling of the SRG, Mamdani's plan would still likely require police officers be present at larger demonstrations. The new civilian-led group, however, would be there to attempt to de-escalate the protesters if it should show signs of turning violent.

Mamdani has not yet said when the civilian-led team will be established or take the streets. He also announced the creation of the First Amendment Support Team (FAST), a 30-officer NYPD unit, that will help deescalate crowds while the CDDD is being developed.

Chell, who has announced he will be running for mayor of New York City in 2029 when Mamdani would likely be seeking a second term, said he doubts the new team will take form until at least 2028 – if ever.

"What's scary about all this, the mentality of how the mayor and his staff and his socialist friends want to run this city," Chell said.

"If they get everything they want, we're gonna be a third world city and I don't know if we can ever get it back."

European-Japanese BepiColombo probe arrives in orbit around Mercury today

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Latest space missions, discoveries and exploration updates.

Stargazing tips, cosmic events and expert insights on the universe

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

It's a moment more than eight years in the making.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Mercury is about to receive a robotic visitor from Earth.

The BepiColombo mission — a joint effort of the European Space Agency (ESA) and the Japanese Aerospace Exploration Agency (JAXA) — will arrive in orbit around Mercury today (Nov. 21), more than eight years after leaving its home planet.

The big moment will come after an orbit-insertion burn, which is scheduled to begin today at 1:24 p.m. EST (1824 GMT) and last for 37 minutes.

BepiColombo consists of two main spacecraft: ESA's Mercury Planetary Orbiter (MPO) and JAXA's Mercury Magnetospheric Orbiter (MMO), also known as Mio.

The duo launched — along with a power and propulsion element called the Mercury Transfer Module (MTM) — aboard an Ariane 5 rocket from French Guiana in October 2018, beginning a long and looping trip through the inner solar system.

Mercury is a difficult planet to reach because it's so close to the sun. Spacecraft approaching the solar system's innermost planet are accelerated to high velocities by the immense gravitational pull of our star and must shed a great deal of kinetic energy to be captured by Mercury's much weaker tug.

So the BepiColombo team designed a trajectory that featured one flyby of Earth, two of Venus and six of Mercury over the course of about seven years. That flight time was increased by a year, however, after the mission suffered an issue with its solar panels, which reduced the power available for the MTM's electric thrusters.

Today's orbital insertion is the latest milestone in BepiColombo's Mercury arrival phase, which is expected to take about six months in total.

Arrival began on Sept. 3, when the MTM released MPO and Mio. The two orbiters remain connected today; they won't separate until Dec. 9 or thereabouts.

Shortly thereafter, Mio will settle into its final orbit, a highly elliptical path that will take it over Mercury's poles. MPO is also destined for a polar orbit, though a much less elliptical one than Mio's; the ESA probe will conduct a series of engine burns before finally getting there on March 10, 2027.

BepiColombo won't start doing science work until April 6, 2027. The two probes will gather complementary data from their two different vantage points, shedding a great deal of light on a mysterious world.

"The mission will study all aspects of Mercury, from the structure and dynamics of its magnetosphere and how it interacts with the solar wind, to its internal structure with its large iron core, and the origin of the planet’s magnetic field," ESA officials wrote in a BepiColombo mission description.

"The data will enable scientists to understand more about the origin and evolution of a planet located close to its parent star, and a better understanding of the overall evolution of our solar system," they added.

Michael Wall is the Spaceflight and Tech Editor for Space.com and joined the team in 2010. He primarily covers human and robotic spaceflight, military space, and exoplanets, but has been known to dabble in the space art beat. His book about the search for alien life, "Out There," was published on Nov. 13, 2018. Before becoming a science writer, Michael worked as a herpetologist and wildlife biologist. He has a Ph.D. in evolutionary biology from the University of Sydney, Australia, a bachelor's degree from the University of Arizona, and a graduate certificate in science writing from the University of California, Santa Cruz. To find out what his latest project is, you can follow Michael on Twitter.

Kieran Gibbs recalls his infamous Chelsea red card

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A case of mistaken identity sparked one of the most bizarre Premier League incidents ever

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Controversial refereeing decisions have been a part of football for a long as the game has been around, but one of the most bizarre incidents in recent memory occurred during Arsenal’s infamous 6-0 defeat to Chelsea in March 2014.

After spotting an Arsenal handball on the goal line, referee Andrer Marriner reached for his pocket and flashed a red card to Kieran Gibbs.

However, the issue here was that it was actually Alex Oxlade-Chamberlain who had handled the ball, and in this pre-VAR era, Gibbs was forced into an early bath, despite the protests of players from both sides.

As Gibbs looks back on the incident more than a decade on, he remembers the confusion, the frustration and the surreal nature of the incident which wouldn’t be allowed to happen these days.

“It feels like a blur now, and it did at the time too,” he reflects to FourFourTwo. “It’s heated in the moment and you’re trying to focus on the game.”

The incident came during a calamitous first half for the Gunners, who found themselves 2-0 down after just seven minutes in what was Arsene Wenger’s 1,000th game in charge of the club.

The handball incident came in the 17th minute, when Oxlade-Chamberlain threw his left hand at an Eden Hazard shot, tipping it around the post. Marriner pointed to the spot and the referee was not for turning, despite widespread protests from both sets of players.

The best features, fun and footballing quizzes, straight to your inbox every week.

The Belgian would slot home the penalty to effectively end the game as a contest, with Gibbs still finding it remarkable that such an incident would occur, given the technology now used in the game.

“It feels crazy that there was no VAR, because you don’t have those kinds of issues anymore,” he continues.

“It was a surreal and very frustrating moment that was unique in the Premier League, so it brought a lot of attention.

“Both captains and the referee were telling me I was definitely going off.

“After a few minutes of protesting, I needed to listen to them.

“It was a big error from the referee, who apologised later and the red card was rescinded.”

For more than a decade, Joe Mewis has worked in football journalism as a reporter and editor. Mewis has had stints at Mirror Football and LeedsLive among others and worked at FourFourTwo throughout Euro 2024, reporting on the tournament. In addition to his journalist work, Mewis is also the author of four football history books that include times on Leeds United and the England national team. Now working as a digital marketing coordinator at Harrogate Town, too, Mewis counts some of his best career moments as being in the iconic Spygate press conference under Marcelo Bielsa and seeing his beloved Leeds lift the Championship trophy during lockdown.

I tested the Xreal Aura glasses for 2 weeks — amazing displays and immersive spatial computing let down by a lack of apps

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Unlock and manage exclusive Tom’s Guide member rewards.

Join the Tom's Guide Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Xreal and Google's first foray into XR glasses is a fascinating look at the future of AR and VR devices. But that also means you're prototyping something that will get better, but maybe not yet.

Why you can trust Tom's Guide Our writers and editors spend hours analyzing and reviewing products, services, and apps to help find what's best for you. Find out more about how we test, analyze, and rate.

For quite awhile I have firmly believed that smart glasses are the next device that will evolve the future of computing, much like the iPhone heralded the next 20 years of tech progression. And this is despite the current "pervert glasses" backlash.

I am fascinated by the sudden influx of AR and XR glasses that offer immersive environments and while also looking "normal." I've already tried Meta VR Glasses and Snap Specs, but neither are actually available for everyone yet. The Google, Qualcomm and Xreal collaboration Aura glasses are.

All three glasses could be considered VR-lite, but I think they're about finding a use between AI glasses and wearing a full headset.

In that way, the Auras are the escaped experiment worth investigating. Following the Samsung Galaxy XR headset, these are just the second device to run Google's Android XR platform natively.

This first stab at an XR headset that I think most people will want is impressive and a great example of what Android XR could be, but it also means you're the guinea pig with all that implies.

Xreal S1 andQualcomm Snapdragon Reality Elite Platform

Right when the Aura launched on November 17, I put together some early impressions of the glassese. For the most part, my thoughts haven't changed. But after using the glasses more, some plusses and minuses have solidified.

I am not an advocate for VR as it currently works with bulky, isolating headsets. However, I can still be impressed virtual reality experiences.

Get instant access to breaking news, the hottest reviews, great deals and helpful tips.

The Xreal Aura provides that with 6DoF spatial computing. The two temple cameras and the nose bridge camera enable pinch gesture controls and you can always use the puck as trackpad, which I defaulted to more often that not.

The pinch controls work well. Occasionally the hitbox was a hair off but I didn't notice lag when I adjusted. I will note that in darker environments, it didn't always work, which isn't totally surprising but something to know.

Out of the box, the Auras come with a variety of mixed-reality experiences, from a VR tour of the Galapagos, to some games and a 360 version of Google Maps that is a delight to explore.

VR enthusiasts will find the 70-degree field of view limiting but I wasn't bothered by it. Watching movies feels like you're in a theater for yourself, and extending my desktop workspace was seamless via the wired accessory cable (or wireless for some devices).

These might be the best displays Xreal has made to date. I tested the refreshed Xreal One Pro Plus a couple of weeks ago and thought it was an improvement, and now the Aura is taking it even further.

Xreal lists the resolution of the Aura's as 1,920 x 1,200 per eye (1200p). It makes for sharp images, especially in games and media. It feels premium.

The color gamut isn't robust and the Aura doesn't offer HDR like the One Pro Plus, but if you don't need that level of display detail, these look good. Plus, with 1200 nits of brightness, the display can handle a number of settings. And the electrochromic dimming gets pretty dark, meaning you should feel more immersed.

Personally, I have never had great luck reading text as is on AR glasses, and while the Aura's are better, you may find text blurry. Fortunately, you can zoom in, which helps.

One advantage the Xreal Aura glasses have over Meta's VR Glasses and the Snap Specs is the built-in Google ecosystem. Snap and Meta have both promised that they're building up their developer connections to create more apps for their glasses, but Google's slew of in-house designs and third-party apps is already robust.

On the productivity side, you've got the entire Google suite from Docs and Drive to your calendar and Gmail. Plus, there's the Google Play Store with normal phone games and a small but growing number of XR-specific apps.

Moving forward, as Android XR grows, this will be what sets the Aura and future Android XR devices apart from Snap and Meta.

Here's the rub: you don't really need XR glasses yet. Xreal's own lineup of AR glasses are already great for productivity, gaming and watching content by connecting to your laptop, mini PC, phone or gaming device.

Because of that, the flaws do stand out a bit more.

If we're thinking of the Aura as VR-lite, especially compared to the Meta VR Glasses, then the biggest missing component for the Xreal Aura glasses is games.

Yes, I did try Demeo, which was fun, and HoloChess, which felt like the Star Wars version of chess. But from what I could tell, there aren't many native XR/VR games on the Play Store, while the Meta Quest Store has hundreds, and Meta promised at least 75 launch titles specifically designed for the VR glasses next year.

You can play 2D games on the Play Store, which I did with some games I already have on my account. Xreal is selling its own first-party controller made with GameSir, but you can use any Bluetooth controller. Again, you can connect other Xreal glasses to your phone, laptop, or gaming device with a controller as well.

It's not clear how long it'll take native Android XR experiences to catch up to Meta, but for now, Meta has the lead.

Xreal rates the battery life of the Aura puck between three and four hours. I found that to be pretty accurate. In my testing watching an hour-long YouTube video and then playing some games for about 20 minutes knocked the battery life from 100% to about 80%.

I debated putting this as a flaw because I'm not sure if you want to keep these glasses on for more than three hours. Perhaps that is the sweet spot. Still, for anything longer, you'll need to recharge.

That said, heat becomes a concern. The puck can get warm, but it does include a fan to dissipate heat. Surprisingly, the glasses can get quite warm, especially in the area that runs across your forehead.

General use seemed okay, but at one point I accidentally turned on screen recording and I could feel the glasses heating up. It never got laptop-panini levels of hot, but as the tech ages, I foresee this being a problem.

The Xreal Auras have just three cameras. Two on the temples and one on the nose bridge. As mentioned, I find the hand tracking pretty decent and pinch gestures work well.

However, I've also worn the Meta VR Glasses, which have 6 external cameras and four internal ones. Fundamentally, Xreal adding seven more cameras would change the design of the glasses, which you can see in the ski-goggle look of Meta's forthcoming platform.

Still, eye tracking makes navigating the XR space better. And compared to Meta, where you can leave your hand on the table or in your lap and still use gesture controls, the three-camera Aura setup is more limiting.

The pushback is the puck trackpad, which I used most frequently. I am comfortable with trackpads and mice, and the puck is a nice midway point.

The puck is a mini-PC with glasses attached, but that doesn't make navigation the best without accessories or the trackpad.

If Xreal's next iteration can add eye tracking or even a couple more external cameras, the already good hand tracking would be even better.

I find the Xreal Aura glasses fascinating, and when its rivals finally launch, I think a new space in the tech world will open up that is more than just pervert glasses and bulky headsets. Perhaps I've given these glasses more grace than future versions will deserve, but for a first go in this space, I'm excited by the potential.

For many, I'm not sure the $1,000+ price tag is cheap enough to dive in, but for the curious and the early adopters, this is a device of potential. It hints at what mixed reality wearables could be.

When I use the Aura, I get what it's supposed to be. It's the taste of a Vision Pro or Quest headset in a lighter, more comfortable package. Xreal has set the bar; it'll be up to everyone to see if they can raise it.

'A future where EVs charge simply by parking': New positioning system could make wireless EV charging much more efficient

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A new positioning technology could make autonomous vehicles and robotaxis more reliable at recharging themselves.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Researchers in Japan have developed a new technology that could enable electric vehicles (EVs) to charge "simply by parking".

Wireless charging is still relatively nascent in the EV market, but scientists hope that integrating more wireless charging devices into roads and parking spaces will move the EV industry away from bulky plug-in infrastructure. It uses the same wireless power transfer technology found in modern smartphones and consumer electronics: a transmitter coil inside in a ground pad generates an oscillating magnetic field, which induces an electric current in a receiver coil underneath the vehicle. This current recharges the EV's battery cells.

However, small misalignments between the two coils can affect how quickly and efficiently the battery recharges. This new system uses short-range radio signals to determine the position of the vehicle's magnetic charging coil relative to the charging device in the ground, including its distance and angle. This helps the EV and its driver get into the optimal position for efficient charging.

The technology could be particularly useful for driverless "robotaxis" and other autonomous vehicles that need to be able to recharge quickly and efficiently without human assistance, the researchers said. They detailed their findings in a study published Aug. 26 in the IEEE Open Journal of Vehicular Technology.

"We hope this research will lead to a future where EVs charge simply by parking, much like smartphones placed on wireless chargers," Ryotaro Hada, first author of the study and a master's student at the University of Osaka's Graduate School of Information Science and Technology, said in a statement.

Existing methods for aligning an EV's charging coil with a wireless charging pad use magnetic sensors or cameras. Magnetic systems work best when the coil is close to the pad, while cameras can struggle in rain, poor lighting or when dirt obscures the pad, the researchers explained.

This new system uses four ultra-wideband radio devices mounted around the vehicle's charging coil to communicate with four antennas around the charging pad.

Get the world’s most fascinating discoveries delivered straight to your inbox.

By measuring how long radio waves take to travel between the ultra-wideband devices and the antennas, as well as detecting tiny differences in how the signals arrive at each of the vehicle's sensors, the system can pinpoint the position and angle of the EV's charging coil relative to the ground pad.

The same technology is used in some digital car keys. They use short radio pulses to detect when the driver is close by, allowing the driver to unlock their vehicle without requiring a physical key.

In laboratory experiments using a scaled-down vehicle model, the system worked out where the vehicle's charging coil was to within 0.09 inches (2.32 millimeters) of its actual position and which direction it was facing to within 0.25 degrees. Correcting for distortions in the radio signals reduced the positioning error by around fivefold, the team said.

The researchers noted that the system still needs to be tested outdoors, on full-size EVs and under real charging conditions.
