Stock Market Today, Nov. 20: Amazon Shares Slide on AI and Mega-Cap Tech Sell-Off

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

On Nov. 20, 2026, Amazon shares fell as investors weighed heavy AI spending and margins amid a broad tech selloff.

By Will Healy – Nov 20, 2026 at 5:30PM EST

Amazon (AMZN -4.10%), a global e-commerce, cloud, and digital advertising platform, closed at $246.82, down 4.10%. Shares fell on Friday amid an AI and mega-cap tech sell-off, while investors are watching capital spending, margins, and AWS growth.Trading volume reached 72.1M shares, coming in about 79% above its three-month average of 40.2M shares. Amazon.com IPO'd in 1997 and has grown 251,971% since going public.

S&P 500 (^GSPC -3.70%) closed at 6,910, down 3.7%, while the Nasdaq Composite (^IXIC -5.28%) finished at 22,617, down 5.28%. Among broadline retail and cloud computing peers, Walmart (WMT +1.51%) closed at $127.95, up 1.52%, while eBay (EBAY -1.39%) closed at $115.59, down 1.39%; Walmart's stronger holiday outlook highlighted steadier consumer demand than Amazon.com's pressure points.

Negative sentiment from both its e-commerce and AWS segments seemed to weigh on Amazon stock today.

Soft consumer spending and an increased need for promotional discounting led it to underperform retail counterparts like Walmart, which, as previously mentioned, rose in today's trading session.

Moreover, capital expenditures (capex) continue to weigh on the stock. The company's plan to spend $220 billion has led to free cash outflows, a concerning sign for a company that generated tens of billions in annual free cash flow until recently. Furthermore, despite a massive amount of liquidity, it has had to turn to the debt markets in a rising-rate environment.

Such concerns were not unique to Amazon, and every stock in the Magnificent Seven, which contend with many of the same challenges, dropped today. Additionally, the Anthropic (ANT -8.50%) IPO has put AI in the spotlight this week. That stock has trended down since opening at $111.50 per share yesterday, likely adding to the negative sentiment surrounding AI.

Admittedly, at a P/E ratio of 20, Amazon stock looks like an increasingly attractive buy, arguably making it the one hyperscaler through 2030 that investors should buy and hold. Nonetheless, with Amazon stock caught in the current downturn, investors should probably move cautiously if they choose to invest.

People Are Complaining About The Secret Lives Of Mormon Wives Spinoff (But I Get It, It's Weird)

Fans Were Speculating About Val’s Relationships On DWTS After Suspension. What An Insider Said

Wait, The Actual Contestants On The Mob Weren't Told The Rules?

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

Sometimes a text can cause a lot of drama.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The Secret Lives of Mormon Wives: Orange County, on the 2026 TV schedule, only just debuted as the first spinoff, and immediately had its own drama. Funnily enough, the biggest drama of the season involved O.G. Mormon Wives star Mayci Neeley, who got dragged into the mix when older sister McCall DaPron clashed with some of the new cast members.

Things quickly escalated when McCall made Mayci aware of some drama with co-star Bobbi Althoff, resulting in a text to Jen Affleck saying, “I’m gonna f*** them up,” in regard to the individuals who clashed with her sister. And then, Jen told everyone what Mayci said. After it all became public, it was suddenly so much bigger than just venting between sisters, making someone who had previously been thought of as one of the most grounded and level-headed of the original Mormon Wives series come across like a mean girl.

I’m sure we’ve all said things in a text in the heat of the moment, but Mayci’s words got out for all to see, leading her to realize her mistake in responding in the heat of the moment. She also expressed real regret for having made the statement at all. In a recent apology statement, she said:

To be honest, I’m embarrassed. I’m genuinely embarrassed that I said that. I don’t remember the intention behind it because I know I wasn’t trying to get into a street fight, like a physical fight. It’s genuinely embarrassing. I don’t know why the hell I said that.

Even though the text did make me look at Mayci a bit differently from the impression I’d gotten of her on The Secret Lives of Mormon Wives, I also understand wanting to defend a family member and don’t blame her for having a strong reaction to feeling like her sister was being attacked. It all felt a bit middle school, and I’m certainly not making ay excuses for her over-the-top response, but the firsthand experience with some wild drama on her own reality TV series probably led Mayci to have a more heightened reaction than she might otherwise have had.

Anytime you get a group of people together who come from different backgrounds, have them spend long periods of time together, and point cameras at them, you’re bound to get some drama as they get to know each other. It was a bit unusual that this particular drama came from someone not actually in the room where it happened, but it sounds like Mayci has definitely learned a lesson from the fallout. She went on to say:

I am very sorry I said that. I did regret saying that. That is definitely out of character for me. … I’m just going to be accountable for the things that I was upset with myself for. I’ve apologized for it, and moving on.

Largely because of Taylor Frankie Paul, we’ve become accustomed to Mormon Wives being synonymous with drama, especially with the chaos surrounding production of the original series. After the cancellation of her season of The Bachelorette, custody issues with her children, and her announced departure, it immediately turned attention to the new spinoff and the drama in the OC. So, I’m glad that Mayci didn’t seem to waste any time in sharing such an honest reaction to her own behavior.

While we don’t yet know the future of this new series, fellow Mormon Wives star Whitney Leavitt is getting her own reality show. You can check out The Secret Lives of Mormon Wives and its Orange County spinoff with a Hulu subscription while we await news of future seasons, other possible spinoffs, and more headline-making drama.

10 Best Easter Eggs in GTA 6

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Aden Carter is a Contributor at DualShockers who has been covering games professionally since 2020. He specializes in lists and guides, with a focus on RPGs, horror games, survival horror, Soulslikes, cozy/sim games, and action-adventure titles. Before joining DualShockers, Aden contributed to GameRant, XDA, Gamepur, I

The most common thing for any game developer to do is to make nods to different franchises in their game. Affectionately called easter eggs, these little details are meant to be surprises for players who love various other franchises.

Whether you are a fan of games, television shows, or movies, there’s plenty to discover in Grand Theft Auto 6. Rockstar has taken the time to hide trophies all around Leonida for those who are diligent enough to go searching for them. Even 'Weird Al' Yankovic is hiding in this game somewhere.

Have you found Dexter’s apartment? Maybe you have gone searching for the literal easter egg. Either way, these are amazing nods to other franchises in the mighty GTA 6.

Dexter is a very popular television series about a vigilante serial killer. This serial killer just so happens to live in Miami, which just so happens to be the city that Vice City is based on. What a coincidence!

It just so happens that Rockstar noticed this connection and decided to add a few nods to the series in GTA 6, with the first of them being Dexter’s apartment, which is located on Washington Island.

Of course, you won’t be able to reach this area right away, but once you get access to the map, you can head straight there to check it out and pay tribute to the television series. You can also find some tools on the roof.

Bigfoot has decided to make his way out of the northern forests and headed south to the marshlands of Leonida. It’s a bit weird that he decided to make this island paradise his home, but I won’t question that.

If you progress through the story of GTA 6, there is a mission that will set you on the path to discovering Bigfoot, but he is easy to overlook, as he runs away rather quickly once you get near him.

I barely caught a glimpse of him, but chased him down and caught a photo before he mysteriously disappeared into the marsh. I have no idea if I will see him again, but it was cool to catch a glimpse of this mysterious figure.

After Grand Theft Auto 5's release, the Mount Chiliad mystery was one of the most talked about easter eggs out there. Here's what we know today.

Some developers make nods to different games or pop culture, but Rockstar takes the time to put a literal easter egg in its game. That’s right, if you go to the correct spot on the map, you can find a nest with literal easter eggs in it.

The location in question is a radio tower in South Vice Dale County. The radio tower takes multiple minutes to climb, but the payoff is definitely worth it, as you find a nest full of easter eggs at the top.

While I won’t spoil what happens when you throw one, it does involve a cutscene, and you should definitely take the time to visit the location and watch it unfold.

One of the most memorable missions in GTA 5 is when Michael’s boat gets stolen and you need to chase down the people responsible. Unfortunately, Michael’s boat doesn’t get returned, but we now know the whereabouts of it, and it turns out that it landed in Jason’s yard.

Next to Jason’s house, there is a run-down boat that looks similar to Michael’s. People have pointed this out for a while, but there was no confirmation until now.

Since the game is officially out, you can check out the boat, and you can even climb on top of it. Once there, you can use your phone’s camera to check out the display. Doing so will allow you to see a display that shows part of the GTA 5 map, seemingly confirming that this is Michael’s boat.

Have you ever heard of a movie called Island Claws? If not, you aren’t the only one, but there is a nod to this film in GTA 6. The movie is from the 1980s, and it is about a scientist who genetically modifies crabs to make them grow larger to solve world hunger.

Unfortunately, this doesn’t work out very well, and a similar thing has transpired in Leonida. In Pelican Valley, you can find a trail of blood that leads to a shack. Inside the shack, you can find information about failed experiments on crabs.

Along with this, Jason will get attacked by a giant crab that jumps down on him from the ceiling. It is a rather large easter egg that is meant to pay homage to the classic independent film. Good job doing your research Rockstar.

One of the things that people loved to do in Grand Theft Auto 5 was break into the military base, and the go-to means of breaking into the base was via the hill jump just outside the fence along the highway.

Well, there is another military base in GTA 6 and it is located near where you start the game. It is called Mile Zero Air Station, and it has a convenient hill just outside the fence that you can use to jump into the base just like in GTA 5.

There is no way that this is a coincidence because the hill is an obvious jumping point. Thank you Rockstar for including an obvious homage to the previous game and the silly antics players got into.

From drawings in a mental hospital to TV and radio commercials, these are nine Rockstar Games Easter Eggs that hint at a shared universe.

The most common meme that has ever been spread about a video game is probably the, “We got [insert item here] before GTA 6.” It popped up every single time something major happened before the release of the game. Well it can’t be said anymore since the game is out.

With that being said, the meme will probably become about either Elder Scrolls 6 or GTA 7, or maybe even RAC 7. You might be wondering what that is. Well, if you scroll through your phone’s Snapmatic app, you can find a post about a game called Barnyard Blue: Hot Pursuit.

The post reads, “Barnyard Blue video game before Race and Chase 7 is crazy.” There is even a comment that reads, “We really got Barnyard Blue games before RAC 7.” This is, of course, a jab at the classic meme that appeared for so many years.

The X-Men actually exist in the Grand Theft Auto universe, except they don’t exist in the same way as you know them. Instead of being called the X-Men, they are referred to as the RX-Men. A strange choice, but one that makes it obvious that it is a reference to the classic hero team.

There are various magazines that you can find in the different convenience stores around Leonida, but you can reliably find this magazine in the 24-7 supermarket in northern Key Lento.

In this story, you can find a copy of RX-Men issue 13 titled, “Paging Dr. Placebo.” It features GTA renditions of different heroes and villains. There’s also collectible cards for these characters sprinkled around Leonida.

Speaking of trading cards, you can find various wrestling trading cards across Leonida. This is an obvious easter egg to the various wrestling trading cards that already exist. The wrestling company is called B.A.M, and it stands for Backyard American Mania.

Florida has a rather large wrestling scene, with the WWE training facility being in Orlando. On top of this, various famous wrestlers live or lived in Florida.

To go even further, the acronym of B.A.M. can also be related to Bird Azarian Memorial Training Center, which is located in Illinois, but is a wrestling training center. Odds are, it is just a reference to WWE wrestling.

Early in the game, Jason and Cal sit down to play some games on the JoyStation, with the system itself being an obvious reference to the PlayStation 5. However, that isn’t the whole easter egg, as the game that is played is called Tenebrae Lux.

Tenebrae Lux is a game themed after the movie called The Lighthouse, which features both Robert Pattinson and Willem Dafoe. This is awesome, but there is yet one more easter egg hidden here.

The name Tenebrae Lux is Latin for the term “light-dark.” This happens to be the exact translation of the French term Clair Obscur, which is one of the greatest games to have been released in recent years and a game that took the world by storm.

This list delves into some of the best Easter eggs in video games, exploring how to unlock them, what they're referencing, and more.

AI adoption could disproportionately harm Black and Latino workers

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

It has become increasingly clear that the AI revolution is not just poised to upend white collar jobs, despite what public discourse might suggest. While entry level roles are, in fact, on the decline in tech and finance—and employers continue to use AI as a rationale for layoffs—there is ample evidence that rapid AI adoption could interrupt pathways for workers without college degrees. 

The effects of this upheaval will be uneven, which means certain demographic groups are more likely to be impacted. Some of the jobs that have been deemed “highly exposed” to AI, including clerical and administrative roles, are largely dominated by women, and a new report from the McKinsey Institute for Economic Mobility posits that Black and Latino workers could also face major consequences.

The report finds that Black and Latino workers are overrepresented in the sectors where automation and AI adoption will supplant work altogether, rather than augment work. While the overall rate of AI adoption is relatively stable across different demographics, Black and Latino workers will be affected more acutely by job displacement. Across working hours that are impacted by AI adoption, 40% of hours performed by Black workers and 41% by Latino workers will be displaced (as compared to 37% among white workers.) 

That may not seem like a huge gap. But as the report points out, many of the jobs that are most exposed to AI have historically been pathways to the middle class, making them crucial drivers of social mobility across communities that have faced systemic barriers to entry. Black workers, in particular, dominate six jobs that are likely to see the biggest decline in labor demand, according to McKinsey, including postal mail carriers and customer service representatives; Latino workers are also especially likely to hold some of those jobs, namely dishwashers and hand packers. 

Black and Latino workers—along with those in rural regions—are also much more likely to be in jobs that involve physical labor, which are more vulnerable to being automated through robotics and other AI-enabled systems. On the whole, lower wage workers will face the greatest impact and are 7.6 times as likely to need to transition into a new job when compared to higher wage workers; people without a college degree are also 1.8 times more likely to need to do the same. 

Meanwhile, white workers are better positioned to both avoid this level of AI disruption and actually benefit from AI adoption in a number of ways. It’s not just that they’re more likely to hold jobs where AI will likely enhance their work, boosting productivity rather than replacing employees outright. When compared to their Black and Latino counterparts, white workers are also 30-50% more likely to work in the industries where private capital will be deployed, yielding more jobs and wealth creation, the McKinsey report states. 

That means white workers already have more of a foothold in jobs involving robotics or semiconductors or biotech, setting them up for success as hundreds of billions of dollars pour into those industries.(Look no further than the Anthropic IPO this week, which has created thousands of millionaires.)  In other words: Without broader policy changes or reskilling initiatives, many Black and Latino workers may be both forced out of their jobs and locked out of opportunities to cash in on the AI boom.

BigBear.ai vs. Innodata: Which Technology Stock Is a Better Buy for 2027?

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

One is built on federal and defense contracts, the other on training AI models for big tech, and their financial profiles diverge sharply.

By Sara Appino – Updated Nov 20, 2026 at 5:29PM EST

10 stocks we like better than BigBear.ai ›

Choosing between these specialized AI plays requires balancing high-stakes government work against commercial data engineering growth. Investors must decide if BigBear.ai (BBAI -8.67%) or Innodata (INOD -8.91%) better suits their portfolio needs.

BigBear.ai provides complex decision intelligence tools for federal and defense agencies, while Innodata helps major tech firms train generative AI systems. Both companies operate in the rapidly evolving world of artificial intelligence but serve different markets. This comparison examines their financial health, customer concentration, and valuation to help you decide which stock fits your strategy.

BigBear.ai offers decision intelligence solutions that combine data from different sources to help leaders make faster choices. It serves critical sectors like national security, cybersecurity, and global supply chains. According to its latest annual report, a specific contract accounted for nearly 51% of consolidated revenue. Customer concentration like this adds a layer of risk to the business.

In the 12 months ended Sept. 30, 2026, revenue reached nearly $142.1 million. This represented a revenue growth decline of approximately 1.5% compared to the prior period. The company reported a net loss of roughly $180.2 million during this same time frame.

Following its early 2026 debt conversion, the debt-to-equity ratio is approximately 0.0x, meaning the company carries virtually no debt relative to its equity. The current ratio, which measures the ability to pay short-term debts with short-term assets, is close to 1.8x. In the fiscal year ended Dec. 31, 2025, free cash flow was negative at nearly $46.3 million.

Innodata operates as a global data engineering firm that prepares and evaluates data for large-scale AI systems. The company serves several high-profile technology leaders, with one customer in its digital data solutions segment accounting for nearly 58% of total revenue in 2025. This focus on the best small cap tech stocks in the data sector highlights its role in the generative AI boom.

In the 12 months ended Sept. 30, 2026, revenue reached approximately $350.2 million. This reflects a revenue growth increase of nearly 46.9% over the previous year. The company also achieved a net income of roughly $48.5 million during this period.

As of its December 2025 balance sheet, the current ratio is approximately 2.7x. The debt-to-equity ratio is 0.0x, indicating a strong balance sheet with no total debt. In the fiscal year ended Dec. 31, 2025, free cash flow was nearly $35.6 million. Note that stock-based compensation represented roughly 23.8% of operating cash flow, which inflates reported cash generation since SBC is a non-cash expense added back in the cash flow statement.

BigBear.ai faces significant risk due to heavy revenue concentration with government customers who can terminate contracts for convenience. Scaling its sales efforts remains a challenge while managing long and unpredictable sales cycles. It also faces intense competition from Microsoft (MSFT -2.77%) and Amazon (AMZN -4.10%). The company also navigates complex AI deployment risks and recent financial volatility including litigation over accounting errors.

Innodata relies heavily on a single customer that contributed nearly 58% of its 2025 revenues, making it vulnerable to contract changes. It competes against well-resourced firms like Accenture (ACN +1.19%) and Cognizant Technology Solutions (CTSH +3.11%) in the data engineering space. The business also deals with geopolitical risks from its global workforce and ongoing legal challenges regarding securities class actions.

Innodata appears to offer a more attractive value proposition based on its lower P/S ratio, which measures market value against sales over the past twelve months.

Valuation metrics include sourcing from Financial Modeling Prep (FMP) and may differ from other data providers.

I'd go with Innodata. AI models are only as good as the data they learn from, and Innodata helps big tech companies create and test that data. Its sales have grown year over year for 13 straight quarters, and it makes money.

Last quarter, sales jumped 53% and management raised its growth outlook, though the stock has fallen sharply this fall. There are risks: Two customers make up most of its sales, margins narrowed, and one program is winding down. But Innodata is adding new customers and work from a large cloud company, and expects to replace the lost program by early next year.

To give BigBear.ai its due, it does high-stakes work for national security and border security customers with contracts that are difficult to replace, and its backlog is growing. But it's still losing money, and government contract sales can be difficult to predict. That uncertainty is where Innodata stands apart: It is already profitable, with plenty of cash to keep investing as demand for AI data grows.

Texas Democrats propose bills to nix state DEI ban at public colleges

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Let Higher Ed Dive's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The party clinched the state’s House and governorship in the midterm elections, though that still may not be enough to get the legislation passed.

In the state’s House, Reps. Nicole Collier and John Bucy III have put forward bills that would repeal the 2023 law. Texas state Sen. José Menéndez introduced a companion bill in the state’s Senate.

At any other time in the past three decades, the new bills might have faced little chance of passage simply by virtue of being championed by Democrats. But the party now controls two-thirds of a trifecta for the first time since 1996 — a makeup that may still not be enough to pass the legislation.

Senate Bill 17 — the target of the new bills — bans several DEI activities at public colleges, including:

Texas was one of the first states to ban DEI offices and initiatives in higher education. Following SB 17 and a similar law in Florida, universities from Ohio to Iowa to Alaska shuttered their DEI offices, often under legislative mandates.

Institutions quickly responded to SB 17. For instance, the University of North Texas said before the law took effect that it would restructure its Multicultural Center and Pride Alliance into the student affairs office and launch a new Center for Belonging and Engagement.

After passage, the Texas A&M University System issued guidance stating that DEI terminology couldn’t be used on the institution’s websites. Offices dedicated to student affinity communities would have to close, though it said some programs and libraries could be absorbed into broader offices and initiatives.

Texas Tech University also closed its DEI office.

Texas Tech University System’s latest chancellor — Brandon Creighton,  who helped author SB 17 — has gone even further than the bill’s requirements, which don’t cover academic content. The system has moved to eliminate academic programs focused on sexual orientation and gender identity and put restrictions on teaching about race, sex and gender in courses.

State audits so far have determined that the University of Texas System, nearly all of the institutions within theTexas A&M University System and 19 of the state’s community colleges are compliant with SB 17.

Audits last year deemed two institutions noncompliant — a community college because it made a new employee participate in a DEI training and Texas A&M-Central Texas because it struck a contract that didn’t conform to the law’s ban.

SpaceX's Starshield troubles in Ukraine could threaten its broader military business

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

“Explain the changes that were made to the Starshield service in October 2026.”

The representative in the US House expected to chair the House Intelligence Committee in January, Jim Himes (D-Conn.), has threatened to withhold support for funding government-owned systems from SpaceX amid the ongoing inquiry into the use of Starshield in Ukraine.

“SpaceX’s potentially unilateral modification of a U.S. Government contract, combined with its lack of responsiveness to congressional oversight, calls into question every contract the U.S. Government has with SpaceX,” Himes wrote in a letter to SpaceX founder Elon Musk on Friday.

CNN first reported the letter’s existence. The network also reported that Himes sent a classified communication to Director of National Intelligence Jay Clayton with a similar message that it would be difficult for Congress to authorize funding for systems with SpaceX as the primary vendor.

SpaceX has developed the consumer Starlink service over the last decade, which provides high-speed Internet from low-Earth orbit to user terminals on Earth. For government customers, SpaceX has also developed Starshield, a suite of satellite services that includes a militarized version of Starlink communications. The US government has purchased Starshield services and extended access to Ukraine, although the terms governing that access are largely classified.

During the last several weeks, a controversy has erupted over interruptions to Starshield service in parts of Ukraine, cutting off connectivity to drones used to defend against Russian attacks.

On October 19, for example, Ukraine President Volodymyr Zelenskyy said of Starshield on social media, “Unfortunately, we have established that the equipment stops functioning at speeds of 130–161 kilometers per hour in various sectors of the front. This is slowing down our frontline logistics and limiting the use of various types of drones, particularly mid-strike drones and interceptors of jet-powered ‘shaheds.’” Additional disruptions were reported a week ago.

The central question is who decided to curtail the performance of Starshield assets in Ukraine. Accounts have variously implicated Musk, SpaceX, the Pentagon, and the White House. Musk has said that US officials control Starshield access, while defending SpaceX’s right to restrict the use of commercial Starlink service in war.

“My ask to you is straightforward,” Himes wrote to Musk. “Explain the changes that were made to the Starshield service in October 2026, why those changes were made, and whether those changes were made unilaterally by SpaceX or were directed by a U.S. Government official.”

The communications from Himes suggest that Congressional Democrats, who will control the House and Senate in January following decisive victories in the Midterm elections, are prepared to not just investigate the disruption of these SpaceX services in Ukraine, but to take action that could threaten SpaceX’s broader national security business.

“It is difficult for me to continue supporting the authorization of funding for these SpaceX-developed, government-owned systems, until I receive satisfactory answers to my questions about the Starshield service in Ukraine,” Himes wrote in his letter to Musk.

Various options are open to Congressional Democrats, as well as some Republicans who have raised similar concerns, including demanding classified briefings and documents and restricting funding through appropriations to the US Department of Defense and other federal departments. Notably, the US Space Force awarded SpaceX a $2.29 billion contract earlier this year to develop the Space Data Network Backbone intended to support the Golden Dome missile defense initiative. That program, along with SpaceX’s work building intelligence satellites for the National Reconnaissance Office, could become a focus of congressional scrutiny.

It seems unlikely that the US government would sever ties with SpaceX for space-based data services or launches, as the company is currently providing capabilities that no other US company can match. But Democrats are clearly serious about putting restrictions on Musk’s ability to influence geopolitical conflicts that have traditionally been the purview of governments rather than corporations.

After all, you don’t need to know everything, only what’s important.

New York’s Luxury Rental Market Sees Influx of Cash from AI Boom

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The artificial intelligence boom has expanded from the rolling hills of San Francisco and Silicon Valley to the busy streets of New York City, where an already tight luxury market is feeling the impact.

The AI wealth boom was in the spotlight this week, following Anthropic’s highly anticipated IPO and jaw dropping $1.494 trillion valuation. With the tech constantly growing and producing newly minted millionaires, the impacts of deep-pocketed AI professionals entering the New York real estate market are just beginning to be felt, agents there say.

There are more than 2,000 AI companies operating in New York City, according to the city’s Economic Development Corporation, and AI companies signed leases for about 1.5 million square feet in just the first six months of 2026, the Brooklyn Daily Eagle reported.

Major AI frontrunners like Anthropic, Palantir, OpenAI, and EliseAI have opened offices in New York, contributing to the thriving commercial real estate market.

“It’s been pretty unbelievable, what’s happening in the public sphere of the AI business. We’re seeing a ton of AI leasing activity on the ground in New York,” Evan Kleinberg, co-founder of Cornelia Street Partners, a boutique commercial real estate firm, told Realtor.com. “And it’s been happening so fast, it’s almost representative of the technology itself. The companies hire immediately and they need space yesterday.”

While Kleinberg has been keeping eyes on the AI industry for the past couple of years, his brokerage worked with its first AI specific company about 12 months ago, at the end of 2025. Since then, the brokerage has secured leases for five companies developing proprietary AI models.

There are a number of reasons Kleinberg sees AI companies expanding east and planting roots in New York. The city has fully rebounded from the pandemic and has an attractive lifestyle that draws many young professionals. Plus, companies see the benefit of being close to their portfolio businesses.

“Ultimately, if your goal is to raise money from Goldman Sachs or JP Morgan or one of the major private equity groups like Apollo, they’re all here,” Kleinberg explained. “So wanting to get close to them is an underlying reason to move.”

With these new offices comes a hoard of young, newly wealthy professionals who are looking to build a home in New York, many of whom are stepping into an already fiercely competitive luxury real estate market.

“Wall Street has historically been a major driver of Manhattan luxury real estate, and now we’re really starting to see the effects of AI. It’s becoming an important engine for wealth creation and housing demand,” Michelle Griffith, a residential real estate agent in New York City, told Realtor.com. “Right now in Manhattan, we are already in a very tight rental market. Even for the last year, rents are up anywhere from 10% to 20% in Manhattan, especially for these luxury properties.”

As of November, the median sold price of a home in Manhattan is $1,150,000, a 15% year over year increase. Some Manhattan neighborhoods have had more extreme changes.

The Lower East Side, for example, saw the median sale price jump 28% year over year to $1,435,000. Rent in the Lower East Side also increased 11% year over year, for a median of $5,947 per month.

The New York City luxury residential landscape is no stranger to competition, with deep pocketed masses vying for a severely limited inventory of spaces. With the flood of AI professionals, another layer of people has been added to the market.

Griffith recalled a recent listing at 20 Gramercy Park North, which had historically rented for $30,000. While a rental like this, which is in the higher end of the market, would previously take 30 to 60 days to identify a tenant, the listing had eight rental bids after 48 hours on the market, with half above asking.

Most workers are determined to live close to work, making it especially challenging to secure an apartment in these highly sought after neighborhoods.

OpenAI’s 90,000-square-foot New York office is located in the Puck Building, which sits on the boarder of SoHo and Nolita in Manhattan.

Anthropic’s New York hub takes up the entirety of a 16-story building at 330 Hudson Street in the Hudson Square neighborhood of Lower Manhattan.

“The offices are in fantastic locations, so it’s easy to find great housing within walking distance,” said Griffith. “The challenge is finding that apartment because there is very, very limited inventory because those neighborhoods were already popular and in demand.”

For luxury agents, the challenge is no longer finding tenants with the money to rent high priced rentals, its finding the rentals for deep pocketed tenants.

When Griffith began in real estate 15 years ago, an apartment for $100,000 per month might be the biggest rental of the year. Now, rentals for $80,000 to even $150,000 are common and moving quickly, she shared.
